It's pretty frustrating because jobs are starting to get all mixed up, so I figured I needed to come in and figure out what's going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you struggled with this kind of distractibility and difficulty staying on task since you were a child, or is this a relatively new problem for you?\n\nPatient: No, it's definitely not a childhood thing. It's relatively new. Um, I'd say this has been building up for about seven months now. It really started right around the time we took on the mortgage for the workshop and all the new equipment. Before that, I didn't have this kind of issue.\n\nDoctor: Since this started seven months ago, would you say you have been experiencing excessive worry or tension about a variety of things, such as your finances or the business, on most days?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, obviously taking on a big loan is a serious thing, but I wouldn't say I'm stressed or worrying about it all the time. I'm not really an anxious guy. It's not like I'm sitting around every day feeling tense about the money or the business failing... it's really just that I can't seem to keep my head straight when I'm actually trying to get the work done.\n\nDoctor: Over the past seven months, have you felt down, depressed, or noticed that you've lost interest or pleasure in doing things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I haven't been feeling down or anything like that. If anything, I've got plenty of energy for the things I like doing. I still enjoy my work and my hobbies, I just... I just can't seem to focus on them. So, no, I wouldn't say I've been depressed or lost interest.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed if your ability to focus changes at all depending on whether or not you have had any alcohol or other substances?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed any difference. I don't drink much anyway, maybe a beer here and there, and I don't use any other stuff. So, I wouldn't say that has anything to do with it. The focus issue is just kind of there regardless.\n\nDoctor: Have you been told by anyone that you snore loudly or stop breathing while you are asleep?\n\nPatient: No, nobody's ever told me that. I mean, I don't think I'm a snorer, and I definitely haven't heard anything about stopping breathing or anything like that. So... yeah, as far as I know, I sleep just fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other physical symptoms or changes in your health, such as headaches, vision problems, or any unusual physical sensations, since these focus issues began?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. No headaches or vision issues, nothing unusual physically. Honestly, physically I feel pretty normal. It's just... just the focus thing, really.\n\nDoctor: Since these focus issues started seven months ago, have you noticed any changes in your personality, such as acting more impulsively or having difficulty with social interactions?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I haven't been acting out or doing anything crazy impulsively. And talking to people at work or whatever is the same as it's always been. So, no, no real changes in my personality or how I act around others. It's just the trouble focusing that's been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulty with your memory, such as forgetting where you put your tools or forgetting important instructions for a job?\n\nPatient: No, my memory's been fine. I haven't been losing my tools or forgetting how to wire things up or anything. I remember what I need to do for work just fine. It's really just getting my brain to stay on the task that's the problem, not remembering the task itself.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any head injuries or concussions in the last year, even if they seemed minor at the time?\n\nPatient: No, no head injuries. I work on construction sites, so I wear a hard hat, but... no, I haven't hit my head or had a concussion or anything like that. Not in the last year, not ever, really.\n\nDoctor: When you say the focus issues began right around the time you took on the mortgage and new equipment, did you feel that those new responsibilities specifically changed the way you have to work or the pressure you feel while working?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. The work itself is exactly the same—wiring is wiring. And as for pressure... I mean, sure, I know I have to pay the bills now, but I don't feel any different while I'm actually on the job. It's not like I'm stressing out about the mortgage while I'm trying to work. It's just normal life stuff, you know? That's why I don't get why my focus is suddenly so bad.\n\nDoctor: Have you seen a primary care doctor recently for a general physical check-up or any blood work to check for things like vitamin deficiencies or thyroid issues?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't been to a regular doctor in a while. I probably haven't had a physical or any blood work done in... maybe a few years? I just haven't really had a reason to go since I usually feel fine physically.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any tingling, numbness, or a 'pins and needles' sensation in your hands or feet?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. My hands and feet feel completely normal, no tingling or numbness or anything.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I just can’t keep my focus on one estimate or code form anymore. \n\nIt's like every little thing pulls me off track. I'll be trying to concentrate, and then I'll hear a tool noise, or I'll get a text, or I'll just suddenly have some new idea about the workshop, and I completely lose what I was doing. It's pretty frustrating because jobs are starting to get all mixed up, so I figured I needed to come in and figure out what's going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you struggled with this kind of distractibility and difficulty staying on task since you were a child, or is this a relatively new problem for you?\n\nPatient: No, it's definitely not a childhood thing. It's relatively new. Um, I'd say this has been building up for about seven months now. It really started right around the time we took on the mortgage for the workshop and all the new equipment. Before that, I didn't have this kind of issue.\n\nDoctor: Since this started seven months ago, would you say you have been experiencing excessive worry or tension about a variety of things, such as your finances or the business, on most days?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, obviously taking on a big loan is a serious thing, but I wouldn't say I'm stressed or worrying about it all the time. I'm not really an anxious guy. It's not like I'm sitting around every day feeling tense about the money or the business failing... it's really just that I can't seem to keep my head straight when I'm actually trying to get the work done.\n\nDoctor: Over the past seven months, have you felt down, depressed, or noticed that you've lost interest or pleasure in doing things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I haven't been feeling down or anything like that. If anything, I've got plenty of energy for the things I like doing. I still enjoy my work and my hobbies, I just... I just can't seem to focus on them. So, no, I wouldn't say I've been depressed or lost interest.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed if your ability to focus changes at all depending on whether or not you have had any alcohol or other substances?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed any difference. I don't drink much anyway, maybe a beer here and there, and I don't use any other stuff. So, I wouldn't say that has anything to do with it. The focus issue is just kind of there regardless.\n\nDoctor: Have you been told by anyone that you snore loudly or stop breathing while you are asleep?\n\nPatient: No, nobody's ever told me that. I mean, I don't think I'm a snorer, and I definitely haven't heard anything about stopping breathing or anything like that. So... yeah, as far as I know, I sleep just fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other physical symptoms or changes in your health, such as headaches, vision problems, or any unusual physical sensations, since these focus issues began?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. No headaches or vision issues, nothing unusual physically. Honestly, physically I feel pretty normal. It's just... just the focus thing, really.\n\nDoctor: Since these focus issues started seven months ago, have you noticed any changes in your personality, such as acting more impulsively or having difficulty with social interactions?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I haven't been acting out or doing anything crazy impulsively. And talking to people at work or whatever is the same as it's always been. So, no, no real changes in my personality or how I act around others. It's just the trouble focusing that's been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulty with your memory, such as forgetting where you put your tools or forgetting important instructions for a job?\n\nPatient: No, my memory's been fine. I haven't been losing my tools or forgetting how to wire things up or anything. I remember what I need to do for work just fine. It's really just getting my brain to stay on the task that's the problem, not remembering the task itself.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any head injuries or concussions in the last year, even if they seemed minor at the time?\n\nPatient: No, no head injuries. I work on construction sites, so I wear a hard hat, but... no, I haven't hit my head or had a concussion or anything like that. Not in the last year, not ever, really.\n\nDoctor: When you say the focus issues began right around the time you took on the mortgage and new equipment, did you feel that those new responsibilities specifically changed the way you have to work or the pressure you feel while working?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. The work itself is exactly the same—wiring is wiring. And as for pressure... I mean, sure, I know I have to pay the bills now, but I don't feel any different while I'm actually on the job. It's not like I'm stressing out about the mortgage while I'm trying to work. It's just normal life stuff, you know? That's why I don't get why my focus is suddenly so bad.\n\nDoctor: Have you seen a primary care doctor recently for a general physical check-up or any blood work to check for things like vitamin deficiencies or thyroid issues?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't been to a regular doctor in a while. I probably haven't had a physical or any blood work done in... maybe a few years? I just haven't really had a reason to go since I usually feel fine physically.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any tingling, numbness, or a 'pins and needles' sensation in your hands or feet?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. My hands and feet feel completely normal, no tingling or numbness or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your balance or any unsteadiness when you are walking?\n\nPatient: No, my balance is fine. I haven't had any trouble walking or feeling unsteady at all.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I just can’t keep my focus on one estimate or code form anymore. \n\nIt's like every little thing pulls me off track. I'll be trying to concentrate, and then I'll hear a tool noise, or I'll get a text, or I'll just suddenly have some new idea about the workshop, and I completely lose what I was doing. It's pretty frustrating because jobs are starting to get all mixed up, so I figured I needed to come in and figure out what's going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you struggled with this kind of distractibility and difficulty staying on task since you were a child, or is this a relatively new problem for you?\n\nPatient: No, it's definitely not a childhood thing. It's relatively new. Um, I'd say this has been building up for about seven months now. It really started right around the time we took on the mortgage for the workshop and all the new equipment. Before that, I didn't have this kind of issue.\n\nDoctor: Since this started seven months ago, would you say you have been experiencing excessive worry or tension about a variety of things, such as your finances or the business, on most days?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, obviously taking on a big loan is a serious thing, but I wouldn't say I'm stressed or worrying about it all the time. I'm not really an anxious guy. It's not like I'm sitting around every day feeling tense about the money or the business failing... it's really just that I can't seem to keep my head straight when I'm actually trying to get the work done.\n\nDoctor: Over the past seven months, have you felt down, depressed, or noticed that you've lost interest or pleasure in doing things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I haven't been feeling down or anything like that. If anything, I've got plenty of energy for the things I like doing. I still enjoy my work and my hobbies, I just... I just can't seem to focus on them. So, no, I wouldn't say I've been depressed or lost interest.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed if your ability to focus changes at all depending on whether or not you have had any alcohol or other substances?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed any difference. I don't drink much anyway, maybe a beer here and there, and I don't use any other stuff. So, I wouldn't say that has anything to do with it. The focus issue is just kind of there regardless.\n\nDoctor: Have you been told by anyone that you snore loudly or stop breathing while you are asleep?\n\nPatient: No, nobody's ever told me that. I mean, I don't think I'm a snorer, and I definitely haven't heard anything about stopping breathing or anything like that. So... yeah, as far as I know, I sleep just fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other physical symptoms or changes in your health, such as headaches, vision problems, or any unusual physical sensations, since these focus issues began?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. No headaches or vision issues, nothing unusual physically. Honestly, physically I feel pretty normal. It's just... just the focus thing, really.\n\nDoctor: Since these focus issues started seven months ago, have you noticed any changes in your personality, such as acting more impulsively or having difficulty with social interactions?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I haven't been acting out or doing anything crazy impulsively. And talking to people at work or whatever is the same as it's always been. So, no, no real changes in my personality or how I act around others. It's just the trouble focusing that's been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulty with your memory, such as forgetting where you put your tools or forgetting important instructions for a job?\n\nPatient: No, my memory's been fine. I haven't been losing my tools or forgetting how to wire things up or anything. I remember what I need to do for work just fine. It's really just getting my brain to stay on the task that's the problem, not remembering the task itself.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any head injuries or concussions in the last year, even if they seemed minor at the time?\n\nPatient: No, no head injuries. I work on construction sites, so I wear a hard hat, but... no, I haven't hit my head or had a concussion or anything like that. Not in the last year, not ever, really.\n\nDoctor: When you say the focus issues began right around the time you took on the mortgage and new equipment, did you feel that those new responsibilities specifically changed the way you have to work or the pressure you feel while working?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I haven't been losing my tools or forgetting how to wire things up or anything. I remember what I need to do for work just fine. It's really just getting my brain to stay on the task that's the problem, not remembering the task itself.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any head injuries or concussions in the last year, even if they seemed minor at the time?\n\nPatient: No, no head injuries. I work on construction sites, so I wear a hard hat, but... no, I haven't hit my head or had a concussion or anything like that. Not in the last year, not ever, really.\n\nDoctor: When you say the focus issues began right around the time you took on the mortgage and new equipment, did you feel that those new responsibilities specifically changed the way you have to work or the pressure you feel while working?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. The work itself is exactly the same—wiring is wiring. And as for pressure... I mean, sure, I know I have to pay the bills now, but I don't feel any different while I'm actually on the job. It's not like I'm stressing out about the mortgage while I'm trying to work. It's just normal life stuff, you know? That's why I don't get why my focus is suddenly so bad.\n\nDoctor: Have you seen a primary care doctor recently for a general physical check-up or any blood work to check for things like vitamin deficiencies or thyroid issues?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't been to a regular doctor in a while. I probably haven't had a physical or any blood work done in... maybe a few years? I just haven't really had a reason to go since I usually feel fine physically.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any tingling, numbness, or a 'pins and needles' sensation in your hands or feet?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. My hands and feet feel completely normal, no tingling or numbness or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your balance or any unsteadiness when you are walking?\n\nPatient: No, my balance is fine. I haven't had any trouble walking or feeling unsteady at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any periods where you feel mentally 'foggy' or confused, beyond just the trouble with focusing on your tasks?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I haven't felt confused or foggy at all. It's more just... having a hard time staying on one task, but my head is clear otherwise. I know what's going on around me, so no, no real confusion.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any new changes in your skin, such as it becoming unusually dry, or have you been feeling much colder than you used to?\n\nPatient: No, my skin seems fine. I haven't noticed it getting dry or anything like that. And I haven't been feeling cold, either. Everything feels pretty normal in that regard.\n\nDoctor: Have you made any significant changes to your diet, such as becoming vegetarian or vegan, in the last year?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't changed my diet at all. I eat the same stuff I always have, so no vegetarian or vegan things for me. Just normal food.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling physically restless, like you have an internal need to be constantly moving or doing something, even when you are trying to sit still?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. I've just been going nonstop lately. I'll go straight from job sites, to doing bids, and then right back to the workshop to rewire test panels. I keep starting these new home-automation ideas before the last one is even done. Sitting still just feels like I'm wasting time, you know? So I just have to keep moving and getting things done.\n\nDoctor: During these periods where you feel like you have to keep moving and starting new projects, do you also feel unusually upbeat, overly talkative, or feel like your mind is racing faster than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah, for sure. I'll get in this really big, pumped-up mood and be talking a lot—like telling everyone how our little shop is about to become the main solar outfit in town. My mind is definitely going fast. \n\nBut... I guess I also get pretty on edge with it. Like, if my wife or one of the apprentices questions the plan, I can just flip. I end up getting really sharp with them over basically nothing. So, yeah, I feel upbeat and talk a lot, but I snap at people pretty easily too.\n\nDoctor: Looking back over your life, before this seven-month period started, have you ever had other times where you felt this same 'pumped-up' way, with racing thoughts and a need to keep moving, that lasted for a few days or longer?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I've always been a hard worker and I've had good days where I get a lot done, sure. But... nothing like this. \n\nThis whole feeling of being totally pumped up and not being able to stop my brain or my body for days at a time, that's definitely new. I really haven't had anything like this happen before these last few months.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
